ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-10-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u117	Thank you very much. The following is a presentation of the views of the groups of Members. Mag. Bojan Krajnc will present the position of the Group of the Party of the Modern Centre.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-10-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u118	Thank you for your word, Mr. Vice President. Dear Secretary of State, distinguished colleagues and colleagues! The Law amending and supplementing the Law implementing the customs rules of the European Community follows the amendments adopted at European Union level since the last amendments to the Law in 2007 in the field of customs legislation and following amendments to national legislation in the field of customs rules. For example, the EU Regulation has entered into force the European Community Customs Code and the Financial Administration Act has been amended in its domestic legislation. The law thus includes a change in the use of the terms "customs administration, the General Customs Office and the Customs Office" and replaces them throughout the text with the terms "financial administration, the General Financial Office and the Financial Office". The law so clearly defines that the financial administration is the authority that performs the duties and obligations of the customs authority in accordance with the customs legislation of the European Union, international treaties and other legislation which refers to the customs authorities. The law repeals the obligation to carry out specific professional checks to obtain a licence for representation before the customs authorities, but that provision does not apply until 1 May 2016 when the Union Customs Code which directly regulates the business of representation for the so-called authorised economic operators enters into force. The law also regulates e-commerce with financial administration in respect of the performance of customs formalities in such a way that the conditions and rules of operation are laid down by the Minister responsible for the finance. The power to adopt such a provision shall be conferred on the Implementing Regulation of the Union Customs Code. In accordance with the provisions of this Regulation, the customs authorities shall determine the conditions and method for the exchange of data which are standardised messages agreed between Member States, thereby ensuring the interoperability of the customs information systems of the Member States of the European Union. Furthermore, the law provides for the deletion of provisions on customs measures in respect of infringements of intellectual property rights, providing in the transitional provisions for their reasonable application until the application of the national regulation implementing the EU regulation on the enforcement of intellectual property rights by customs authorities. The provisions relating to the sale of goods in cross-border shops are amended and supplemented by reflecting the actual situation after the entry of Croatia into the European Union and, at the same time, the measures of customs control of frontier shops with a view to increasing clarity, transparency and ensuring adequate control are combined in one place. The Government states in its proposal or explanation of the law that there will be no need to provide additional financial resources for the implementation of the law, as the funds for the implementation of the law are provided in the State budget under the financial plan of the Financial Administration of the Republic of Slovenia. In the SMC Group, we will support the proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Law on the implementation of the customs rules of the European Community. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-10-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u119	Thank you very much. Ms Susana Lepi Šimenko will present the position of the Slovenian Democratic Party's Group of Ambassadors.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-10-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u120	Thank you for your word, chairman. Hello again! Slovenia entered a single customs area at the time of entry into the European Union, a space based on the free movement of goods, services, capital and people. The area of free movement of goods is governed by the uniform rules of the customs union, which must also be applied in Slovenia. The single common customs rules apply directly, meaning that their transposition into national law is not necessary, but that Member States have the possibility of their own arrangements for certain areas, so we have a special law in Slovenia that specifically regulates these areas. This is the law we are talking about today, the law implementing the customs rules of the European Community. The law entered into force in 2004 and was completed in 2007. This time, too, it is only a partial reform of the law, while a comprehensive reform of the law, which will be fully in line with the new customs law of the Union, can not be expected until early 2016. Among other amendments to the proposed law is that all those who have the status of authorised economic operator will be able to carry out representation operations in all EU Member States. Since Slovenia is one of the countries that requested specific professional checks for representation before the customs authorities, it must abolish this licence in accordance with European law. A number of changes in the law relate to editorial corrections relating to the change in the use of the concepts of customs authorities with the concepts of financial authorities, which are linked to the previous reorganisation of customs and tax administrations. However, most of the amendments to the proposed law relate to the sale of goods in border stores, where it is actually a matter of alignment with the real situation, which should have been amended after Croatia's entry into the European Union. Although we regret in the Slovenian Democratic Party's Group that we have only minor changes to the law before us and that we do not have a law before us that fully takes account of changes at EU level. However, we agree with the proposed amendments to the law, so we will support the law implementing the customs rules of the European Community. Thank you.

The provisions relating to the sale of goods in frontier shops are amended and supplemented to reflect the actual situation with regard to the entry into the EU of the Republic of Croatia. At the same time, customs controls of border stores are combined in one place with a view to increasing clarity, transparency and ensuring, in particular, adequate control over the operation of border stores. However, users will be allowed to access in the most friendly manner the information they need to carry out customs formalities before the customs authorities. Special attention and great effort have been made in the formulation of provisions that build on the restriction on the establishment of companies. The main purpose of the provision is to try to prevent as many unfair business practices as possible related to the play of the establishment of new companies. Third time. In addition, provisions to improve corporate governance and corporate law are being built up. Criminal sanctions are also being renewed in order to ensure that the level of penalties depends on the size of the company. Fourth time. We support the amendments tabled by the coalition groups exempting the Republic of Slovenia and the companies associated with the management of its assets from restrictions of establishment. In accordance with Rule 138 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, I propose that a third consideration of the proposed law be held at this meeting of the National Assembly. We also support the extension of the limitation deadlines for companies which are majority owned by the State or local community. ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-10-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u178	Honourable President of the National Assembly, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! The concept and the proposal of the law before you did come to the government by the Ministry of Justice, but it was created as a joint project by the Ministry of Finance, the Ministry of Infrastructure, the General Secretariat of the Government, the Ministry of Justice and the Government Services. The proposal for a law before you is, without being dramatic, the first attempt in the history of independent Slovenia in order, following the successful example of Iceland, which is also in the National Assembly, or Ireland, to establish a legal basis for the investigation of a bank hole for systematic investigations or audits of irregularities in the implementation of major investment projects financed by taxpayers. Practically all such projects in the past have left behind a bad taste, followed by them, were the result of criminal investigations, the Court of Auditors' audit, the activities of political or parliamentary investigative commissions. The draft law provides such a legal basis for the establishment of specific investigative or audit committees to conduct focused systematic investigations of all stages of planning and the implementation of projects of national interest in which the Republic of Slovenia is directly or indirectly involved. The Commissions will be composed of representatives of the key supervisory institutions of the State, with the responsibility of participating all those involved in the project under investigation in any way and their findings will be presented to the public as soon as possible in the form of a report. It is also worth noting that the law does not provide for the establishment of a new State authority, nor does it constitute competition for existing supervisory and investigative authorities or the institution of parliamentary investigation, but extends and maximizes the use of existing control mechanisms. In other words, this law fills the gap created by other institutional mechanisms. We all know that we have a search for criminal responsibility, that we have the Court of Auditors' audits, that we have the functioning of parliamentary investigative commissions, which we are missing, and that sometimes in large projects, which are "too big to fail", if you allow the use of this to focus on the actual situation, the actual situation that would enable the decision-making of each government to take further decisions to protect public interest. The Commissions of Inquiry, under the proposed law, shall no longer constitute and must not constitute a search for on-call offenders or a determination of criminal liability, although it may also be used under legal conditions for the purpose of determining compensation and criminal liability, of course, when the competent authorities are already in operation. Their operation, the functioning of these committees, is primarily aimed at strengthening existing control mechanisms to identify and remedy systemic deficiencies or at least limit negative effects, as proposed by law. However, the publication of the findings will ensure transparency of their operation and adequate awareness of the general public, which indirectly or directly financed these projects. That's why the law, for example, sets the maximum time for such an investigation to take place for 15 months. The purpose of the bill is to provide, through systematic investigations, quality data on the reasons, causes and backgrounds for cost inefficiencies or cost inefficiencies in the use of public funds in large infrastructure projects or projects of State importance. These are the projects proposed by law whose total value or financial burden the State exceeds 1 per cent of the budget in force of the Republic of Slovenia. Finally, let me stress that the adoption of the law under consideration is another part of the mosaic of more effective prosecutions of corruption and economic crime, which I believe we are all committed here. It's a new concept. It's a new tool. Its use will depend on people who get it in their hands and will be entrusted to them. But I would point out that if they don't get this tool, then there won't be any. There are many questions about what the concept of this law should be, and we welcome this public debate after all. Thank you very much.

The bill under consideration should allow for the rapid and comprehensive implementation of the investigation, preparation and implementation of major projects. It is envisaged that the Government will set up an investigative commission for each case, which will carry out a system-wide investigation within a maximum of 15 months. ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-10-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u182	Thank you very much, Mr. President, for your word, Mr. Secretary, distinguished colleagues and colleagues! After a few hours of deliberation on the proposal for a fiscal rule law, we have before us the proposal for a law on systematic investigations of projects of national importance. The applicant notes that the ownership, management and control structure in the Slovenian economic area is intertwined. Effective, efficient and responsible management of capital investments, other public funds and assets requires the implementation of rules on cost-effective, careful and responsible spending. What will be today's debate? More reserved or more focused on asking questions to yourself and to the proposer? What can we expect from this law? Will he bring the expected results? The bill is the first attempt in the history of Slovenia to establish, in Iceland's successful example, a legal basis for systematic investigations into state investments in larger projects. The proposed law is the legal basis for the establishment of specific investigative committees focusing on the investigation in all the investment phases of the larger state project, i.e. in the planning of the project itself. It is indicated when and on whose initiative the Commission is established. It is determined which of the former distinguished legal, economic, audit or other experts will be elected by the President of the Commission. There are also twelve indents, of which individuals are appointed to the Commission. The Commission shall consist of at least two members in addition to the President, and the maximum number of members of the committee shall not be specified. In fact, it is possible to invite external experts to cooperate if the Commission considers that it needs experts with a certain knowledge that members do not have for a successful investigation. The Commission shall carry out a system-wide investigation in several stages and may also relate to a particular part of the project. It must complete its task within 15 months of its establishment. If the report is not prepared within this period, it may be extended by a maximum of three months. What else does this law provide? How information and documents should be obtained as to how persons are invited to interview, how to cooperate with other national authorities, how to cooperate and exchange data with foreign authorities, how to inform competent authorities and the public, how to limit conflicts of interest, how to protect classified and confidential data, and how to store data and documented material and, last but not least, criminal provisions in the event of non-compliance with the law. Respectable, the proposal for a law is, if measured by the number of articles, short in terms of objectives and objectives, very demanding. Today we will decide that the proposal for a law is suitable for further consideration. The Social Democrats will also confirm this proposal or decision because we supported the 15 points of the Commission to prevent corruption when the members of the Senate opted for a collective protest retreat. We have signed a coalition treaty in which we commit ourselves to investigating a bank hole and to establish liability. Our efforts must go urgently towards restoring confidence in financial institutions as well as in supervisory institutions. We believe that it is necessary to carry out an independent audit of the construction of TES6 because in the document under consideration today, the greatest words are intended for this particular project, a billion and 500 million euros. However, it should be noted that the last rehabilitation of banks and the TEŠ6 project are unfortunately not the only projects that have swallowed millions of euros for citizens and citizens. Before that, there were irregularities, but people were more satisfied with their situation at the time. They had a job and a paycheck. Unfortunately, this people's satisfaction broke out at a time when we began to face the dimension of the economic and financial crisis. Because of the crisis, irregularities that have taken place in companies and banks have been on the surface. Dear ones, we like to imitate others, tell others many times how they do and do things. This bill also compares with other countries, including Iceland. In comparison with Iceland, I would like to draw attention to a recent visit by Mrs Eve Joly, a consultant to the Icelandic government to investigate the collapse of the banking system and the fight against corruption in Slovenia. She said that economic crime is difficult to investigate. Bank investigations are not completed overnight, it is necessary to be precise in reviewing credit cards and only then can you get the right result. We understood that in solving known irregularities, you must be firm, determined, and that you must be persistent and immune to threats, pressures, and other ways of influencing the decision - making of investigators. That is why, in the event of the adoption and enforcement of this law, the Social Democrats expect two in particular. First time. The final content of the law should be such as to provide the investigators, on the one hand, with an adequate legal basis for an effective but constitutionally consistent investigation work and, on the other hand, not to distort the rights of those whose actions will be investigated. This means that the rules of investigation will be clear, that there will be no doubt about the powers of those investigating and that the legal situation, rights and obligations of investigators will be clearly defined. Secondly, in the event of a clear legal regime, the work of the investigators will be carried out in a committed, objective and consistent manner with the principles of integrity. If both conditions are met, then the law will achieve its fundamental purpose, otherwise only a dead letter could remain on paper, which I believe no one wants.

With regard to the bank hole, I would first like to mention some facts that are worth repeating over and over again. The banking crisis in Slovenia did not come out of the blue, but it was the result of the world capitalist crisis, the largest since World War II. This crisis has been deeper and longer with us and across southern Europe than it could have been, and this is due to the functioning of the euro area, which benefits the centre's countries at the expense of the periphery. The crisis could not have been avoided by Slovenia, but it could have been mitigated if the government had implemented anti-cyclical measures before the crisis and if the government that ruled during the crisis had recapitalised banks in due time. You can read more about this in the Bank of Slovenia report on the causes of the capital deficit. I'm mentioning all this because we need to be aware of what this investigation can and will not reveal. The structural causes of the crisis are a matter of scientific analysis, while the investigation can only show whether individuals who have made political and business decisions have made economic and legal decisions. In short, we do not expect much from searching through credit maps, which the government obviously relies on, but we are particularly interested in the decisions of the Government and Benke Slovenije in autumn and winter 2013 regarding bank rehabilitation. This recovery has been suspicious since the beginning. Let's take a look at some data. Firstly, the difference in the assessment of the bank's capital deficit following the two assessment methods used was about 45%. For comparison in Spain, where similar stress tests were carried out in 2012, there was virtually no difference, which is normal because the method from below serves only to check and calibration or repair the method from above. Of course, 45% is no calibration, but a sign that there is something very wrong with the assessment. The methodology of the asset quality review, which showed negative capital in NLB and NKBM, although a few months earlier the review in accordance with accounting standards showed positive capital, was also never explained. On this basis, small shareholders have been disbanded. Both stress tests in 2013 and 2014 used extremely strict assumptions about economic trends in Slovenia, although newer, more encouraging data and so-called dynamic method were available. So far, all these procedures have been investigated and assessed only by the Government and Banka Slovenije, which is why the audit is necessary. I think there was something wrong here, because it's getting worse. Today our banks are too capitalized. NLB had 22.7% capital adequacy and NKBM had 25.8%. By comparison, Raiffeisen International from Austria promises to absorb capital adequacy from 10% to 12% by 2017. Therefore, in the United Left, we expect the investigation that will be carried out under this law to find the culprits for these decisions, whether it be business or political. We expect the same in the case of TEŠ 6. Political responsibility is already recognised by the parliamentary investigative commission requested in the United Left. In doing so, we expect that the findings to be made by the Commission of Inquiry, together with the conclusions to be reached by the special investigative group of the Government, will provide a clear overview of all involved, and that it will be clear at last who, when and where, through political ties and pressures, has reached the end of the project and that the cost of its implementation from the initially estimated 600 million euros has increased to astronomical 1.4 billion euros. That we urgently need a legal basis that will allow for an independent forensic investigation, we have been alerted in the United Left since day one. We therefore welcome the response of the Government, which established the working group last November, and the draft law prepared by this working group is being discussed today. The working group was composed of representatives of the Cabinet of the President of the Government, the Ministry of Infrastructure and the Government's Law Service, and the Government's decision was also open to the participation of external experts, representatives of the Court of Auditors, the KPK and other state bodies. Given that the proposed law also aims to ensure transparency in investigative projects, we are interested here in who these external experts were and in which representatives of the Court of Auditors and the Commission for the Prevention of Corruption or other bodies participated in the drafting of the law. But we also have some problems with just the content of the bill. The biggest and very obvious problem that law enforcement authorities have been alerting since the start of these projects investigations is that banks and others do not want to cooperate and do not wish to provide them with the required documentation, which makes it impossible for them to carry out investigations effectively. We therefore wonder how the Government intends to force these same banks to provide documentation that they do not wish to provide to the National Investigation Office to this special government commission. We also wonder whether it is appropriate to propose a very narrow circle of people for the President of the Commission: former Constitutional Judges, former Supreme Judges, former Members of the Court of Auditors, former members of the Bank of Slovenia Council, former members of the Commission for the Prevention of Corruption. This circle is so narrow that we can count potential candidates almost to the fingers of one hand, even if we take into account the legal restrictions on membership. At this point, I could point out that this article could contain names and surnames, but it would be no longer. Nor do the provisions that define when a project of national importance is at all appropriate to us. They should be determined so that they can investigate all the projects at issue and not only those whose value exceeds 1% of the national budget. We believe that the Special Commission could also deal with projects that did not exceed these poor €90 million, and it is also clear that there are suspicions of corruption. You don't have to have a lot of imagination to find out that this is what I'm aiming at, say, at what kind of public health invitation. There was a lot of talk in such projects, and no result. In our view, therefore, the Commission set up under this law must not be only a commission to determine political responsibility and cannot be a commission that is almost identical to the parliamentary inquiry as to its powers and modalities. Further consideration of the law will also need to consider appropriate solutions for the establishment of a commission. If we want all the projects in question to be really investigated, it is necessary to ensure that the establishment of such a commission does not fall within the exclusive competence of the Government. As a result of such a provision, something fast can happen to us that the government will launch a project that will be brought to an end within four years so far that it will no longer be paid to stop it. We've seen this before, and we've heard this phrase several times, even in the case of TER 6. We are therefore proposing that it be clearly determined that the Government should establish a special group if, for example, one third of Members so require. There is a lack of a safeguard that prevents the government from setting up a commission for those projects in which it is naturally involved. This is just a few of the concerns and considerations about the law before us, which, of course, is being corrected in the next phase of the legislative process and can also be improved. We certainly do not want to adopt a law to investigate all projects that are complicated by suspicions of corruption and illegality, and then the findings of this special commission would be similar to those of all the Commission's major reports on anti-corruption in cases where important representatives of the political options involved, as well as the judgments of the courts. We expect the government and the coalition to take further account of our proposals and comments. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-10-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u189	The word is given by the Group of the Modern Center Party, for her mag. Dušan Verbich.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-07-10-SDZ7-Redna-10.ana.u190	Madam Vice President, thank you for your input. Dear Minister, colleagues and colleagues! We have a law before us that regulates the matter that is new to our legal system. This is a new, different approach and a way to detect and prevent corruption in the political and economic sphere and represents one of the extremely important measures and counter-action processes of corruption, including the so-called entanglement of a country by which certain businesses with types of manipulation and illegal actions have an impact on the policy to create normative frameworks that create certain benefits and competitive advantage for these companies over others. What does this law bring, and why is it needed? This law lays down the legal basis for enabling mutual cooperation to date more or less between isolated existing supervisory and investigative authorities, which are limited in their procedures to powers and tasks, and in practice for certain state investment projects, such as the construction of the TES 6 block or the bank hole, have proved to be flawed and incomplete in the comprehensive disclosure of suspected irregularities and non-economics and the identification of the causes and factors for them, which have also been resounded by the media. It is a matter of making it possible to carry out a systematic investigation and audit of major state projects in a comprehensive manner and to assess, within one year or no later than 15 months plus 3, both in terms of the cost-effectiveness and regularity of the planning, decision-making and implementation of the project as well as the prevention of negative consequences for the budget of the Republic of Slovenia. The subject matter of the investigation is large state-of-the-art projects where there is serious doubt as to the regularity, economy, whether the initial or further investment of the project is appropriate. The investigation or audit will be carried out by an investigative commission whose members will be from supervisory authorities or other public sector entities, as well as other private sector experts. It is important that membership is voluntary, meaning that public authorities such as Banka Slovenije may refuse membership if they consider that cooperation would affect impartiality, the independence of their original tasks or would prejudice the interests of the primary procedure. The Commission will not identify individual criminal liability, but in the event that infringements are detected during the course of the investigation, it will respond appropriately and give incentives to the competent institutions to carry out their procedures. The Commission will be able to cooperate with other national supervisory authorities, financial investigation bodies, for taxes, to prevent money laundering, and will be able to obtain data and documentation which the Authority may also reject for reasons of reason. The Commission may invite and conduct interviews with individuals who do not have the status of the examiner or the interrogator, but witnesses, which means that they may reject the answers to certain questions and the interview must be answered. Since the Commission is established by the Government, the question of the political motivation and interrelationship of this Commission is rightly raised, but by law the Commission is independent, independent and not bound by guidelines, guidance from the Government or other bodies, and the Government does not have the power to resolve an investigation into a particular matter, since it would interfere with the independence and independence of the Commission. It is therefore a matter of creating an autonomous and independent expert commission, and how effective it will be, it will show time. A very important factor in following the rule of law and limiting corruption is also the transparency of the work of the commission of inquiry, which is ensured through the permanent publication of the report on the government's website. The question is whether existing supervisory institutions are not effective enough to adopt this law and if they are not, why should we not change the competence of existing institutions? Where, then, is the added value brought about by this law? When it comes to large investment state projects that exceed one percent of GDP in Slovenia, which may affect the stability and efficiency of the financial system of the country, and there is a suspicion of the correctness and cost-effectiveness of spending this money, at any stage of the project, it is right that the Government of the Republic of Slovenia, as a good and prudent owner, is watching over these projects and in such cases activates the ad hoc investigative commission. So a group of experts from different fields and with a wide range of knowledge that can be fully integrated in one place with the exchange of mutual knowledge and the common objective of a much more extensive and efficient investigation and revision of business than can be carried out to a limited extent by individual institutions. For example, the Court of Auditors may carry out an audit by law only for the users of public funds or for the auditor himself, but it does not apply to other stakeholders who have had a significant impact on the decision to build the TES6 block. These are private companies and individuals who have participated in all stages of the preparation and implementation of the project. Moreover, it does not have the power to carry out inquiries and to obtain notices and documentation from other persons who have cooperated after this project and individuals who are not employed in the audited companies at the time of the audit. Nor can they interview and request information from persons who are not responsible at the time of the audit, but in the case of TEM 6, many decisions were taken outside TES and HSE on the basis of non-disclosure, unlawful influences in corporate authorities, enforcement and legislative bodies. It is therefore not possible to expect from this limited audit of the Court of Auditors that their findings will allow for the full disclosure of the reasons for the state of the project and the reasons for the ineffective and unsuccessful corporate and ownership control of the implementation of the TEM 6 block project. The adoption of this law establishes an interdisciplinary team composed of experts from different fields, and together they work together to resolve the common problem of specific expertise. This will make it easier to explore and identify a comprehensive picture of the actual situation, assess procedural irregularities, cost inefficiencies of the project, fail to control the financing of the company and identify backgrounds, and the reasons for failing to control the financing and management of projects, e.g. As mentioned, in the case of the construction of the TES 6 block, and exceptional non-economic incentives to the taxpayers. The law will strengthen the supervisory mechanism, which will also act preventively and in future it will be easier to prevent the occurrence of such cases as TEM 6: for example. The second railway track, third development axis, electronic tolling, investments in health etc.The legal basis is to find proposals for a system to improve the implementation of a specific project, but can also propose legislative changes and measures to prevent systemic, procedural and cost irregularities of future major projects. [[…]] In identifying bad business practices and finding solutions to eliminate harmful methods of political-economic interplay and interference, it is of paramount importance that the joint action of experts of different disciplines can, in a preventive sense, become a transdisciplinary team that together exceeds the conceptual and methodological limits of its disciplines and find new solutions to problems beyond existing ones. This is a process that poses a major challenge and, comparatively, with the countries of Ireland, Iceland, Great Britain, Austria, we can see that the responsible executive branches of authorities are somehow deciding on such steps. The proposed law provides the basis for us to build on Iceland and Ireland's existing system, to establish control mechanisms to deal with suspicious and non-economic major state projects, to identify and identify key actors, and not least to prevent the causes or patterns of network operations that, from political-clientistic or private interests, with the non-economic use of public money to the detriment of taxpayers, take advantage of opportunities to gain private profits and benefits. If we fail to uncover the way in which TES 6 works and identify the lobby they have led, there is a major recurring danger. The bill represents a major step towards preventing systemic corruption, characterised by the corruption of political officials and the impact of economic enterprises on political decisions, resulting in adjusted legislation, the capture of public money flows and public sectors of the economy. The functioning of the administrative and political apparatus becomes dependent on political authorities, which also control key segments of the economic system. As a response to increasing systemic corruption, there is in practice a clear lack of tools to reduce or even prevent it, and in view of the existing political-economic intertwined mechanisms of informal networks. By adopting the proposed law, the legal basis for greater preventive action and accession to systemic corruption will be established, the establishment of an investigative commission will strengthen the country's supervisory functions and will also improve the relationship of authorities with supervisory authorities. However, the SMC has already committed itself to the objective of comprehensive investigation and prevention of the corruption system in the pre-election period. That is why the bill has our support. Thank you very much.

This means, in other words, that Article 2 of the Constitution, which guarantees social rights, is above Article 148 of the Constitution, which lays down a fiscal rule or otherwise; the State is first obliged to provide for social rights and only then for the Stability and Growth Pact, which is defined here as some other inter-State agreement. That is why we hope that the Charter of the United Nations will be adhered to and that we will incorporate this safeguard into fiscal rule so that the social rights of citizens and citizens will not be jeopardised by a fiscal rule. In the United Left, of course, I will support this amendment.

And so today we are in the final stage of the implementation of the Treaty. What is the purpose of the contract? The aim of the Treaty is to strengthen the economic pillar of the Economic and Monetary Union with a set of rules to strengthen budgetary discipline, by fiscal strengthening, strengthening economic policy coordination and improving the governance of the euro area, while at the same time supporting the objectives of ensuring sustainable growth in employment, competitiveness and social cohesion. This is the content of the contract, as well as the content of today's fourth phase of the implementation of the fiscal pact. That's why we need this. The country needs this document.
